# Semantic Stage 1 Semantic Query API

## Scope
- Add the Stage 1 `semantic_query.h` foundation from `docs/plans/lsp/01-semantic-inference-core.md`.
- Affected layers: parser public API, semantic query implementation, compiler frontend semantic diagnostics publication, parser semantic test registration, LSP semantic query consumption, and semantic documentation.

## Baseline
- `SZrSemanticContext` already stored expression, reference, numeric, reachability, logical, and ownership facts.
- Callers had to know the individual fact APIs directly; there was no shared parser-level query facade for LSP/debug/REPL.
- The initial `Diagnostics` query returned only a valid empty list; CFG reachability facts were not yet exposed as structured query diagnostics.
- Before the compiler publication slice, `compile_script` could emit reachability facts during type inference, but it never queried `ZrParser_SemanticQuery_Diagnostics`; `SZrSemanticContext.queryDiagnostics` therefore stayed empty after successful compilation.

## Test Inventory
- `tests/parser/test_semantic_query.c`
  - `TypeAt` returns the narrowest matching expression type.
  - `FactsAt` aggregates expression, numeric, reachability, logical, and ownership facts at a position.
  - `DefinitionOf` maps a resolved read reference to a matching declaration reference by `symbolId`.
  - `DefinitionOf` prefers a reaching write fact when a resolved read carries `definitionRange`.
  - `DefinitionsOf` returns multiple reaching write facts when a read carries `definitionRanges`.
  - `ReferencesOf` collects scoped references for a symbol and clears reused output arrays before a miss.
  - node scope filters out facts outside the root node range.
  - `Diagnostics` returns a valid empty list when no diagnostic facts exist.
  - `Diagnostics` maps an in-scope `UNREACHABLE` reachability fact to one structured `unreachable_code` warning and ignores reachable or out-of-scope facts.
  - `Diagnostics` maps in-scope read facts with definite-assignment state to `uninitialized_read` / `possibly_uninitialized_read` diagnostics and ignores initialized or out-of-scope reads.
  - `Diagnostics` consumes `ZrParser_SemanticFacts_ResolveLinearDefiniteAssignments` output, reporting a read before a write and ignoring the read after the write.
  - `Diagnostics` maps in-scope numeric facts with `mayOverflow=true` to `numeric_overflow` warnings and ignores out-of-scope overflow facts.
  - `Diagnostics` maps in-scope computed-member expression diagnostic facts to `array_index_out_of_bounds` errors and ignores out-of-scope bounds facts.
- `tests/parser/test_compiler_semantic_query_diagnostics.c`
  - `compile_script` publishes semantic query diagnostics into `SZrSemanticContext.queryDiagnostics` after compiling a constant ternary branch.
  - `compile_script` also publishes `unreachable_code` when an interval comparison logical fact proves a conditional expression branch unreachable, covering `seed: u8` in `seed < 300 ? 1 : 2`.
  - `compile_script` publishes `numeric_overflow` when existing parser/type-inference facts mark `9223372036854775807 + 1` as `mayOverflow`.
  - `compile_script` publishes `array_index_out_of_bounds` when fixed-size array inference sees a constant computed index outside the array, covering `var values = [1, 2]; return values[2];`.
  - `compile_script` publishes `array_index_out_of_bounds` when fixed-size array inference proves a non-constant index expression's entire numeric range is outside the array, covering `index: u8` with `values[index + 2]`, while the same fixture keeps `values[index]` to prove a partially overlapping range is not diagnosed.
  - `compile_script` publishes warning `array_index_may_be_out_of_bounds` when a fixed-size array non-constant index expression range overlaps legal indexes but also contains invalid values, covering `index: u8` with `values[index]` for a two-element array.
  - `compile_script` publishes error `array_index_type_mismatch` when a fixed-size array is indexed by a known non-integer expression, covering `var values = [1, 2]; return values["name"];`, while keeping bounds diagnostic counts at zero for that fixture.
  - `compile_script` publishes warning `array_index_may_be_out_of_bounds` for a min-only / no finite-upper-bound array when the known integer index range may be negative, covering `var values: int[1 ..]` with `index: int`; the same fixture keeps `index: u8` silent and checks that the warning message does not print `array max size: 0`.
  - Warning-level query diagnostics do not set compiler `hasError` or `hasStructuredError`.
  - CFG-backed definite-assignment joins publish `possibly_uninitialized_read` for `var seed; if (flag) { seed = 1; } return seed;`.
  - CFG-backed reaching-definitions joins clear the single-definition payload for `var seed; if (flag) { seed = 1; } else { seed = 2; } return seed;`, proving the old linear resolver's last-source-order write is not treated as the only reaching definition.
  - CFG-backed reaching-definitions loop-carried coverage clears the single-definition payload when a loop-body read can see both the initializer and a previous iteration write.
  - CFG-backed reaching-definitions constant-true loop coverage preserves the write before `break` as the final read's single reaching definition.
  - CFG-backed definite-assignment constant-true loop coverage suppresses false `uninitialized_read` / `possibly_uninitialized_read` diagnostics after `var seed; while (true) { seed = 1; break; } return seed;`.
  - Compiler-side definition query coverage verifies `compile_script` leaves enough reaching-definition payload in `SZrSemanticContext` for `ZrParser_SemanticQuery_DefinitionOf` to resolve `seed = 3; return seed;` directly to the write token.
- `tests/parser/test_semantic_facts.c`
  - CFG-backed reaching-definitions cloned-finally coverage clears `hasDefinitionRange` when normal and function-exit paths through the same source `finally` read see different definitions.
- `tests/language_server/test_lsp_semantic_query_diagnostics.c`
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes parser semantic query diagnostics through the LSP diagnostic path, covering a constant ternary branch that must produce `unreachable_code`.
  - `GetDiagnostics` also publishes `unreachable_code` for an `if` branch made unreachable by an interval comparison logical fact, covering `seed: u8` in `if (seed < 300) ... else ...`.
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes `numeric_overflow` from the same semantic query diagnostic path for `9223372036854775807 + 1`.
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes `array_index_out_of_bounds` from the same semantic query diagnostic path for a fixed-size array literal indexed at its length.
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes `array_index_out_of_bounds` for the same interval-index fixture and requires exactly one diagnostic so `values[index]` is not a false positive.
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes warning `array_index_may_be_out_of_bounds` for fixed-size array partial-overlap interval indexes and keeps the definite `array_index_out_of_bounds` count at zero for that fixture.
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes error `array_index_type_mismatch` for a known non-integer array index and keeps the bounds diagnostic counts at zero for that fixture.
  - `GetDiagnostics` publishes warning `array_index_may_be_out_of_bounds` for min-only arrays whose known integer index range may be negative, while leaving positive no-finite-upper-bound indexes silent.
- `tests/language_server/test_lsp_reaching_definition_navigation.c`
  - `GetDefinition` consumes parser reaching-definition facts through the LSP path, covering `return seed` after `seed = 3` and requiring the result to point at the write token instead of the declaration token.
  - `GetDefinition` returns both branch write locations after divergent `if/else` branch writes and no longer falls back to the declaration when those reaching definitions are enumerable.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_range_inference.c`
  - Unary `-` over an integer interval flips endpoints with overflow-safe arithmetic, so `seed` registered as `2..4` produces result and numeric fact range `-4..-2`.
  - Unary `-` over `INT64_MIN..INT64_MIN` clears the result range and still records a no-range numeric promotion fact instead of dropping numeric facts entirely.
  - Integer interval comparisons write exact logical facts when the child numeric facts prove the result: `seed < 10` with `seed` range `2..4` records `ZR_SEMANTIC_LOGICAL_FACT_ALWAYS_TRUE`, `seed > 10` records `ZR_SEMANTIC_LOGICAL_FACT_ALWAYS_FALSE`, and `seed < 3` remains unknown because the intervals overlap.
  - Integer `/` over safe intervals records a checked quotient range: `seed` registered as `8..12` in `seed / 2` produces numeric promotion range `4..6`; a divisor interval crossing zero clears the range while preserving the numeric fact.
  - Integer `%` over safe intervals records a conservative remainder range: `seed` registered as `8..12` in `seed % 5` produces numeric promotion range `0..4` with unsigned payload; a divisor interval crossing zero clears the range while preserving the numeric fact, and `INT64_MIN % -1` also clears range.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_branch_refinement.c`
  - True-branch simple integer comparison refinement narrows a registered identifier range in a temporary branch scope: `seed` registered as `0..255` in `if (seed < 10) { seed + 1; }` produces branch expression range `1..10`.
  - True-branch equality refinement intersects the registered identifier range with a singleton: `if (seed == 10) { seed + 1; }` produces branch expression range `11..11`.
  - True-branch edge-inequality refinement applies a single-interval complement only when the excluded point is an interval edge: `if (seed != 0) { seed + 1; }` produces branch expression range `2..256`.
  - True-branch simple logical-AND refinement applies both comparison bounds in one temporary branch scope chain: `seed` registered as `0..255` in `if (seed > 2 && seed < 10) { seed + 1; }` produces branch expression range `4..10`.
  - False-branch simple integer comparison refinement narrows the registered identifier range by inverting the comparison bound in a temporary branch scope: `seed` registered as `0..255` in `if (seed < 10) { seed + 1; } else { seed + 1; }` produces else-branch expression range `11..256`.
  - False-branch edge-equality refinement applies a single-interval complement only when the excluded equality point is an interval edge: `if (seed == 0) { ... } else { seed + 1; }` produces else-branch expression range `2..256`.
  - False-branch inequality refinement inverts `!=` to an exact equality singleton: `if (seed != 10) { ... } else { seed + 1; }` produces else-branch expression range `11..11`.
  - False-branch simple logical-OR refinement inverts both comparison bounds and intersects them in one temporary branch scope chain: `seed` registered as `0..255` in `if (seed <= 2 || seed >= 10) { seed + 100; } else { seed + 1; }` produces else-branch expression range `4..10`.
  - False-branch logical-AND same-direction complement refinement merges touching or overlapping inverted ranges for the same identifier: `if (seed < 10 && seed < 20) { ... } else { seed + 1; }` produces else-branch expression range `11..256`.
  - True-branch logical-OR same-direction refinement merges touching or overlapping true ranges for the same identifier: `if (seed < 10 || seed < 20) { seed + 1; }` produces then-branch expression range `1..20`.
  - Unary logical-not branch polarity refinement delegates supported conditions to the opposite branch scope: `if (!(seed < 10)) { seed + 1; }` produces then-branch expression range `11..256`.
  - True-branch logical-OR disjoint segment refinement preserves a fixed two-segment payload: `seed` registered as `0..255` in `if (seed < 10 || seed > 20) { seed + 1; }` produces envelope `1..256` with segments `1..10` and `22..256`.
  - True-branch nested logical-OR/AND segment refinement first reduces same-identifier child conditions, then preserves a fixed two-segment payload: `if ((seed > 2 && seed < 10) || seed == 20) { seed + 1; }` produces envelope `4..21` with segments `4..10` and `21..21`.
  - Else-if scope stacking preserves the outer false branch and inner true branch ranges: after `if (seed < 10) ... else if (seed < 20) { seed + 1; }`, the inner branch expression range is `11..20`.
  - False-branch logical-AND disjoint segment coverage preserves the fixed two-segment complement payload: `seed` registered as `0..255` in `if (seed > 10 && seed < 20) { ... } else { seed + 1; }` produces envelope `1..256` with segments `1..11` and `21..256`.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_branch_segment_sets.c`
  - True-branch interior inequality hole-set refinement splits a known integer interval into disjoint segments: `seed` registered as `0..20` in `if (seed != 10) { seed + 1; }` produces envelope `1..21` with segments `1..10` and `12..21`.
  - True-branch same-identifier logical-OR segment-set refinement can carry a third dynamic segment: `if (seed < 5 || seed == 10 || seed > 20) { seed + 1; }` produces envelope `1..256` with segments `1..5`, `11..11`, and `22..256`.
  - True-branch same-identifier equality disjunction preserves a six-segment dynamic payload: `if (seed == 1 || seed == 3 || seed == 5 || seed == 7 || seed == 9 || seed == 11) { seed + 1; }` produces envelope `2..12` with segments `2..2`, `4..4`, `6..6`, `8..8`, `10..10`, and `12..12`.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_branch_assignment_dataflow.c`
  - Simple if/else assignment joins same-name numeric assignment RHS ranges for the following expression: `if (flag) { narrowed = 1; } else { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `2..11`.
  - Segment-preserving simple if/else assignment joins keep the disjoint branch-write payload for the following expression: `if (flag) { narrowed = 1; } else { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces envelope `2..11` with segments `2..2` and `11..11`.
  - Nested simple if/else assignment replay joins inner branch writes before the outer branch result: `if (flag) { if (inner) { narrowed = 1; } else { narrowed = 2; } } else { if (inner) { narrowed = 10; } else { narrowed = 20; } } narrowed + 1` produces `2..21`.
  - Simple if/else terminal multi-statement assignment joins same-name numeric assignment RHS ranges when both branch blocks have a preceding expression and a terminal assignment: `if (flag) { flag; narrowed = 1; } else { flag; narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `2..11`.
  - Simple if/else nonterminal multi-statement assignment joins same-name numeric assignment RHS ranges when both branch blocks contain exactly one simple assignment followed by an ordinary expression: `if (flag) { narrowed = 1; flag; } else { narrowed = 10; flag; } narrowed + 1` produces `2..11`.
  - Simple if/then assignment joins the true assignment RHS with the retained false-path pre-branch binding: `narrowed: 5..5; if (flag) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Explicit else-only assignment joins the else assignment RHS with the retained true-path pre-branch binding: `narrowed: 5..5; if (flag) { flag; } else { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Same-target sequential if/else assignment replay joins RHS-dependent branch writes for the following expression: `if (flag) { narrowed = 1; narrowed = narrowed + 1; } else { narrowed = 10; narrowed = narrowed + 1; } narrowed + 1` produces `3..12`.
  - Simple if/else multi-target assignment replay joins cross-target RHS-dependent branch writes for the following expression: `if (flag) { low = 1; high = low + 1; } else { low = 10; high = low + 10; } high + low` produces `3..30`.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_loop_assignment_dataflow.c`
  - Unknown-condition `while` assignment joins the retained pre-loop range with the loop-body integer RHS for the following expression: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` multi-statement assignment replay ignores ordinary expression statements around a simple numeric assignment and joins the retained pre-loop range with the loop-body RHS: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { flag; narrowed = 10; flag; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` multi-target assignment replay joins each replayed target with its retained pre-loop range for the following expression: `low: 5..5; high: 20..20; while (flag) { low = 1; high = low + 10; } high + low` produces `12..25`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` nested simple if/else assignment replay joins the retained pre-loop range with nested branch writes: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { if (inner) { narrowed = 1; } else { narrowed = 10; } } narrowed + 1` produces `2..11`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent increment assignment replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + positive_integer_literal`: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 1; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent decrement assignment replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target - positive_integer_literal`: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed - 1; } narrowed + 0` produces `-9223372036854775808..5`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent singleton delta assignment replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + step` when `step` is a non-target positive singleton binding: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 1..1; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent positive-range delta assignment replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + step` when `step` is a non-target identifier with a strictly positive lower bound: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 1..3; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Init-free/no-step unknown-condition traditional `for` assignment joins the retained pre-loop range with the loop-body integer RHS for the following expression: `narrowed: 5..5; for (; flag; ) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Assignment-init/no-step unknown-condition traditional `for` joins the init-updated zero-iteration path with the loop-body path for the following expression: `narrowed: 5..5; for (narrowed = 1; flag; ) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `2..11`.
  - Non-assignment-step unknown-condition traditional `for` preserves body assignment zero-or-more join when the step is an ordinary expression: `narrowed: 5..5; for (; flag; flag) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Direct `var` init/no-step unknown-condition traditional `for` registers the header-local initializer binding for body replay without leaking it outside the loop: `narrowed: 5..5; for (var step: int = 10; flag; ) { narrowed = step; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Known-false traditional `for` assignment init applies the supported init RHS and skips unreachable body/step replay: `narrowed: 5..5; for (narrowed = 1; false; ) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `2..2`.
  - Unknown-length `foreach` assignment joins the retained pre-loop range with the loop-body integer RHS for the following expression: `items: int[]; narrowed: 5..5; foreach (item in items) { narrowed = 10; } narrowed + 1` produces `6..11`.
  - Unknown-length `foreach` direct item assignment joins the retained pre-loop range with the iterable element range for the following expression: `items: int[]` with element range `2..4`; `narrowed: 5..5; foreach (item in items) { narrowed = item; } narrowed + 1` produces `3..6`.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_loop_self_dependency_dataflow.c`
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent positive expression delta replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + deltaExpr` when `deltaExpr` has a strictly positive inferred int64 lower bound and does not depend on loop-written targets: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 1..3; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + (step + 1); } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent zero-inclusive positive-range delta replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + delta` when `delta` is stable, int64, nonnegative, and positive-possible: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 0..3; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent zero-inclusive negative-range delta replay widens the retained int64 range lower bound for first-write `target = target + delta` when `delta` is stable, int64, nonpositive, and negative-possible: `narrowed: 5..5; step: -3..0; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `-9223372036854775808..5`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent sign-crossing delta replay widens the retained int64 range to the full signed interval for first-write `target = target + delta` when `delta` is stable, int64, and spans negative through positive values: `narrowed: 5..5; step: -3..3; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `-9223372036854775808..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent same-loop-written delta replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + delta` when `delta` is a direct identifier assigned by an earlier supported loop replay step: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 0..0; while (flag) { step = 2; narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent same-loop-written expression delta replay widens the retained int64 range for first-write `target = target + deltaExpr` when every loop-written target inside `deltaExpr` is assigned by an earlier supported loop replay step: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 0..0; while (flag) { step = 2; narrowed = narrowed + (step + 1); } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent zero-only no-op delta replay preserves other assignment replay for first-write `target = target + 0`: `narrowed: 5..5; other: 0..0; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 0; other = 10; } other + 0` produces `0..10`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent zero-only prefix then positive delta replay widens the retained int64 range when an earlier same-target write is only a preserving no-op: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 0; narrowed = narrowed + 1; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent adjacent positive-then-negative net-zero delta replay preserves the retained int64 range when consecutive same-target stable self-dependent steps combine to `0..0`: `narrowed: 5..5; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 1; narrowed = narrowed - 1; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..5`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent target-independent interleaved positive-then-negative net-zero delta replay preserves the retained int64 range when an ordinary middle assignment does not read the same target: `narrowed: 5..5; other: 0..0; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 1; other = 10; narrowed = narrowed - 1; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..5`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent target-reading interleaved positive-then-negative net-zero delta replay preserves the retained target range and replays the intermediate target range for ordinary readers: `narrowed: 5..5; other: 0..0; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 1; other = narrowed; narrowed = narrowed - 1; } narrowed + 0; other + 0` produces target `5..5` and observer `0..6`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent target-reading interleaved positive-then-larger-negative net-negative delta replay widens the retained target range downward and replays the reader envelope: `narrowed: 5..5; other: 0..0; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + 1; other = narrowed; narrowed = narrowed - 2; } narrowed + 0; other + 0` produces target `-9223372036854775808..5` and observer `-9223372036854775808..6`.
  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent same-target sequence replay widens through a later same-loop-written delta after executing the earlier supported replay assignment: `narrowed: 5..5; step: 0..0; while (flag) { step = 2; narrowed = narrowed + 1; narrowed = narrowed + step; } narrowed + 0` produces `5..9223372036854775807`.
- `tests/parser/test_numeric_loop_self_dependency_conditional_delta_dataflow.c`

  - Unknown-condition `while` self-dependent target-reading symbolic signed-additive expression net-zero delta replay preserves the retained target range when supported binary `+`/`-` delta expressions have equal checked signed integer-literal sums and matching signed non-literal terms: `narrowed: 5..5; other: 0..0; step: -3..3; while (flag) { narrowed = narrowed + ((step + 5) - 2); other = narrowed; narrowed = narrowed - (step + 3); } narrowed + 0; other + 0` produces target `5..5` and observer `0..11`.

## Acceptance Decision
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise unary-minus over zero-minus direct-range expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where unary `-` wraps a `zero - direct-range` operand whose left side is proven exact zero and whose right side becomes a valid direct int64 range after existing zero-identity wrapper normalization, while the resulting double-negated range is still consumed only by existing supported zero-identity and signed all-ones helpers.
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise zero-minus direct-range expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where binary `-` has a left side proven exact zero and a right side that becomes a valid direct int64 range after existing zero-identity wrapper normalization, while the resulting negated range is still consumed only by existing supported zero-identity and signed all-ones helpers.
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise unary-minus zero-identity-wrapper operand expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where unary `-` wraps an operand that becomes a valid direct int64 range after existing proven exact-zero identity wrapper normalization, while the resulting range is still consumed only by existing supported zero-identity and signed all-ones helpers.
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise nested exact-zero zero-identity expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where nested `&` consumes one exact-zero operand and a recursively supported zero-identity range operand, or nested `|`/`^` consumes one exact-zero operand and returns the recursively supported zero-identity range operand. Broader signed bitwise algebra, nonzero identities, `zero - span`, nonzero unary minus, arbitrary expression normalization, fixed-point replay, and CFG-wide dataflow remain out of scope.
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise exact-zero unary-minus wrapper expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where unary `-` wraps an operand already proven exact `[0,0]`; broader nested zero-identity range publication, nonzero unary minus, and general signed bitwise algebra remain out of scope.
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise signed all-ones OR annihilator expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where binary `|` has either side proving direct int64 exact `-1..-1` after existing exact-zero identity wrapper normalization, including matching nested top-level exact-zero/zero-identity consumers.
- Accepted for ordinary bitwise signed all-ones mask zero-identity-wrapper expression range facts and LSP local-query facts where binary `&` has one exact `-1..-1` all-ones mask side after proven exact-zero identity wrapper normalization and the other side has a valid direct int64 range, including matching exact-zero top-level consumers.
- Accepted for the bounded seven-additional-level positive singleton scale-product coefficient residual eight-hop ordinary reader chain fixture, parser/LSP semantic-query surface, and no fixed four-hop cap audit for ordinary assignments already collected into the loop-assignment plan.

- Accepted for deterministic source-order ranking of first multi-definition reaching-definition query output.
- Accepted for unary-minus integer interval range inference, endpoint-overflow range clearing, and no-range unary numeric promotion facts at the type-inference fact layer, plus LSP regression coverage of existing interval-addition payloads.
- Accepted for integer interval comparison logical fact emission at the parser/type-inference fact layer, plus LSP local semantic query consumption of the same parser-owned logical fact.
- Accepted for integer division interval range inference at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including safe quotient range propagation, zero-crossing divisor range clearing, and LSP local semantic query consumption of the parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for integer modulo interval range inference at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including conservative remainder propagation, zero-crossing divisor and `INT64_MIN % -1` range clearing, numeric range helper split, and LSP local semantic query consumption of the parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for true-branch simple integer comparison interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including preserving original symbol/type/declaration payloads in a temporary branch scope and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for false-branch simple integer comparison interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including inverted comparison bounds, preserving original symbol/type/declaration payloads in a temporary branch scope, and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for true-branch simple logical-AND interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including sequential comparison-bound intersection, full temporary branch environment cleanup, and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for false-branch simple logical-OR interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including inverted comparison-bound intersection and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for true-branch equality interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including single-point integer range intersection and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for false-branch edge-equality interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including single-interval complement only when the excluded point sits on the current interval edge and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for true-branch edge-inequality interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including single-interval complement only when the excluded point sits on the current interval edge and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for false-branch inequality equality interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including inverting `!=` to a singleton equality scope and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.
- Accepted for splitting numeric branch refinement parser and LSP branch query coverage into focused targets so future branch cases do not keep expanding the generic numeric range/query files.
- Accepted for exact interval logical facts driving constant branch reachability diagnostics in compiler conditional expressions and LSP `if` diagnostics, including code-aware de-duplication so direct analyzer diagnostics do not suppress semantic query diagnostics on the same range.
- Accepted for numeric overflow semantic query diagnostics from existing `mayOverflow` numeric facts through parser query, compiler `queryDiagnostics`, and LSP diagnostics.
- Accepted for fixed-size array constant-index bounds semantic query diagnostics through parser query, compiler `queryDiagnostics`, and LSP diagnostics.
- Accepted for fixed-size array definite interval-index bounds semantic query diagnostics through parser query, compiler `queryDiagnostics`, and LSP diagnostics, including no false positive for partially overlapping `values[index]`.
- Accepted for fixed-size array possible interval-index bounds semantic query diagnostics through parser query, compiler `queryDiagnostics`, and LSP diagnostics, using warning `array_index_may_be_out_of_bounds` for partially overlapping known integer ranges.
- Accepted for primitive integer full-range array index regression coverage and finite max-size array upper-bound semantic query diagnostics, including `arrayMaxSize` / `min==max` upper-bound handling and no positive-index bounds diagnostic for min-only/no-finite-upper-bound arrays.
- Accepted for known non-integer array index semantic query diagnostics through parser query, compiler `queryDiagnostics`, and LSP diagnostics, using error `array_index_type_mismatch` while leaving unknown/no-inferable-range indexes silent.
- Accepted for min-only/no finite-upper-bound array negative-interval semantic query diagnostics through compiler `queryDiagnostics` and LSP diagnostics, using warning `array_index_may_be_out_of_bounds` when a known integer range may be negative while keeping positive no-upper-bound indexes silent.

- Accepted for representable true-branch logical-OR same-direction interval refinement at the parser/type-inference fact layer, including merging touching/overlapping true ranges for the same integer identifier and LSP local semantic query consumption of the refined parser-owned numeric fact.

- Accepted as a support split for coefficient-cross semantic query coverage: the original parser/LSP coefficient-cross targets now cover the base one-layer residual shapes, while `test_numeric_loop_self_dependency_symbolic_coeff_cross_deeper_scale_dataflow.c` covers 32 parser deeper scale-product residual and no-op shapes. LSP deeper coverage is split between `test_lsp_numeric_loop_self_dependency_symbolic_coeff_cross_deeper_scale_semantic_query.c` for general deeper scale-product queries and `test_lsp_numeric_loop_self_dependency_symbolic_coeff_cross_three_additional_scale_semantic_query.c` for the third-wrapper query family; both use `lsp_numeric_range_query_test_support.c/.h`. This does not change accepted Stage 2 semantics; it keeps the widening facts from the preceding coefficient-cross slices independently verifiable.
- Accepted for unknown-condition `while` self-dependent target-reading symbolic negative range-coefficient residual replay at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to non-target same-sign negative int64 range coefficients in the product coefficient slot, so `+(step * factor); reader; +(step + step)` with `factor=-4..-2` keeps a negative `[0..2] * step` residual and widens the target downward.
- Accepted as a support split for LSP self-dependency symbolic query coverage: `test_lsp_numeric_loop_self_dependency_semantic_query.c` now remains below the near-threshold band and the six symbolic exact-net-zero LSP cases live in `test_lsp_numeric_loop_self_dependency_symbolic_semantic_query.c`. This does not change accepted semantic behavior; it keeps future Stage 2 symbolic slices from stacking more responsibilities into the general self-dependency LSP test target.
- Accepted for init-free/no-step unknown-condition traditional `for` numeric assignment zero-or-more range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to the bounded loop assignment replay shapes already accepted for while.
- Accepted for assignment-init/no-step unknown-condition traditional `for` numeric assignment zero-or-more range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to an existing numeric-range target and numeric-range assignment RHS.
- Accepted for assignment-step/no-init unknown-condition traditional `for` numeric assignment zero-or-more range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to an existing numeric-range target, numeric-range assignment step RHS, and bounded body replay.
- Accepted for non-assignment-step unknown-condition traditional `for` body assignment zero-or-more range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to an ordinary non-assignment step expression not blocking bounded body replay.
- Accepted for direct variable-declaration-init/no-step unknown-condition traditional `for` numeric assignment zero-or-more range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to a direct identifier `var` binding with an inferable numeric initializer and bounded body replay.
- Accepted for known-false traditional `for` assignment-init range application at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to an existing numeric-range target and numeric-range assignment RHS with no body/step execution.
- Accepted for folded false-condition traditional `for` assignment-init range application at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to supported assignment init when the condition is deterministically false through logical facts or AST constant boolean evaluation.
- Accepted for false-condition traditional `for` variable-declaration-init scope isolation at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to direct `var` init header bindings not leaking after an unreachable loop.
- Accepted for known-true/omitted no-init/no-step traditional `for` break-prefix at-least-once assignment range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to supported numeric body assignments before a plain `break;`.
- Accepted for known-true/omitted no-step traditional `for` init/header-local break-prefix at-least-once assignment range join at the parser/type-inference and LSP local semantic query layers, limited to supported assignment init or direct identifier variable-declaration init and supported numeric body assignments before a plain `break;`.

The ordinary zero-identity and all-ones mask identity forms preserve `2..3`, the sign-crossing double-negated zero-minus form preserves `-2..3` / `-128..127`, exact-zero `&` publishes `0..0`, and the OR annihilator form publishes `-1..-1`. TDD evidence: WSL gcc parser first failed 6 of 92 cases and focused LSP reported the same six old ranges, with `allOnes | (-(zero - (unit - zero)))` discovered GREEN before production changes; WSL gcc/clang focused parser 92/0 + LSP PASS followed by manually relinking fresh bitwise helper/test objects in the isolated `build-wsl-*-lsp-bitwise-unary-minus` directories because normal target rebuild is currently blocked by unrelated active AOT metadata-remap signature mismatches. Full repository GREEN is not claimed; exact-zero predicate expansion, nonzero unary-minus exact-zero, `unit - nonzero`, nonzero RHS subtract wrappers, nonzero identity wrappers, different-identifier structural equality, general signed bitwise algebra, arbitrary expression/offset normalization, fixed-point convergence, and CFG-wide loop dataflow remain open.

- Stage 1 current clarification: the accepted ordinary bitwise nested exact-zero zero-identity expression/LSP local-query slice covers only nested `&` exact-zero annihilator and `|`/`^` exact-zero identity forms whose nonzero side is already accepted by the current supported zero-identity range consumer. - Stage 1 current clarification: the accepted ordinary bitwise signed all-ones OR annihilator expression/LSP local-query slice covers only binary `|` where either side becomes direct int64 exact `-1..-1` after existing exact-zero identity wrapper normalization. Parser coverage uses `allOnes=-1..-1; zero=0..0; span=-2..3` for `allOnes | span`, `span | allOnes`, `(allOnes + zero) | span`, `((allOnes | span) & (+zero))`, and `(zero + zero) | (span | (zero + allOnes))`; LSP mirrors with `allOnes=0-1` and `span=seed-128` (`-128..127`).
